For my father was a stern man who believed in holding love at arm’s length and measured affection by the depth of awe it inspired.

“ ‘My daughter!’ broke from his lips, and he never inquired who she was or what; no, not even when after a moment of silence she raised her head and with a sudden low cry of passionate longing looked in his face and murmured,

“ ‘I never had a father.’

“Sirs, it is impossible for me to continue without revealing depths of pride and bitterness in my own nature, from which I now shrink with unspeakable pain. So far from being touched by this scene, I felt myself grow hard under it. If he had been disappointed in my choice, queried at it or even been simply pleased at my obedience, I might have accepted the wife I had won, and been tolerably grateful. But to love her, admire her, glory in her when Evelyn Blake had never succeeded in winning a glance from his eyes that was not a public disapprobation! I could not endure it; my whole being rebelled, and a movement like hate took possession of me.

“Bidding my wife to leave me with my father alone, I scarcely waited for the door to close upon the poor young thing before all that had been seething in my breast for a month, burst from me in the one cry,

“ ‘I have brought you a daughter as you commanded me. Now give me the blessing you promised and let me go; for I cannot live with a woman I do not love.’

===Chinese===
「他在床上怎样撑起身来，用怎样急切的眼睛读她年轻的脸，看她纤秀的身影在骤起的激动里怎样朝他摇曳，如微风里的一豆火苗——这些我永远不会忘记。只要我一息尚存，我也忘不了他朝她抬起衰老的双臂时那一阵不能自持的狂喜，忘不了她怎样从我身边一跃而去，偎依——是的，偎依——在那副胸膛上；据我记得，连我最早的童年时代，那胸膛也从不曾为我敞开过。因为我父亲是个严峻的人，他信奉把爱拒于一臂之外，用敬畏的深浅来度量感情。

「『我的女儿！』这一声从他唇间脱口而出；他从不问她是谁、是何等样人；不，连片刻沉默之后她抬起头来，忽然发出一声低低的、满含热望的呼喊，望着他的脸喃喃说出那句话时，他也没有问：

「『我从来没有过父亲。』

「诸位，我再往下讲，便不能不揭出我自己天性中那深藏的骄傲与怨毒；如今想起，犹觉痛不可言。这一幕非但没有打动我，我看着看着，心肠反而硬了起来。倘若他对我的选择失望，对它盘诘，哪怕只是满意于我的服从，我也尽可以接受我赢来的妻子，并且存几分感激。可他竟爱她、赏慕她、以她为荣——而伊芙琳·布莱克却始终不曾从他眼里博得一瞥不是当众贬斥的一瞥！我受不了；我整个的人都反了，一股近乎憎恨的情绪攫住了我。

「我叫妻子出去，让我同父亲单独待着；不等那可怜的年轻人的身影被门掩住，我胸中沸腾了一个月之久的种种，便迸作一声喊喊了出来：

「『我遵命给您带来了一个女儿。现在请您把许下的祝福给我，放我走；因为我没法同一个我不爱的女人过日子。』
===Original===
“Instantly, and before his lips could move, the door opened and the woman I thus repudiated in the first dawning hour of her young bliss, stood before us. My God! what a face! When I think of it now in the night season﻿—when from dreams that gloomy as they are, are often elysian to the thoughts which beset me in my waking hours, I suddenly arouse to see starting upon me from the surrounding shadows that young fair brow with its halo of golden tresses, blotted, ay blotted by the agony that turned her that instant into stone, I wonder I did not take out the pistol that lay in the table near which I stood, and shoot her lifeless on the spot as some sort of a compensation for the misery I had caused her. I say I wonder now: then I only thought of braving it out.

“Straight as a dart, but with that look on her face, she came towards us. ‘Did I hear aright?’ were the words that came from her lips. ‘Have you married me, a woman beneath your station as I now perceive, because you were commanded to do so? Have you not loved me? given me that which alone makes marriage a sacrament or even a possibility? and must you leave this house made sacred by the recumbent form of your dying father if I remain within it?’

“I saw my father’s stiff and pallid lips move silently as though he would answer for me if he could, and summoning up what courage I possessed, I told her that I deeply regretted she had overheard my inconsiderate words. That I had never meant to wound *her*, whatever bitterness lay in my heart towards one who had thwarted me in my dearest and most cherished hopes. That I humbly begged her pardon and would so far acknowledge her claim upon me as to promise that I would not leave my home at this time, if it distressed her; my desire being not to injure her, only to protect myself.

“O the scorn that mounted to her brow at these weak words. Not scorn of me, thank God, worthy as I was of it that hour, but scorn of my slight opinion of her.

“ ‘Then I heard aright,’ she murmured, and waited with a look that would not be gainsaid.

“I could only bow my head, cursing the day I was born.

===Chinese===
「说时迟，那时快，他的嘴唇还未及翕动，门开了，那个被我在她年轻幸福方才破晓的时辰便如此休弃的女人，已站在我们面前。我的上帝！那是一张怎样的脸！如今每逢夜半想起——当我忽然从梦中惊觉，那梦纵然阴郁，比起醒时缠着我的种种念头，竟还算得乐土——看见那张年轻姣好的额头，那一环金发的光晕，自四围的暗影里向我逼视，被那使她霎时化为顽石的苦痛涂没，是的，涂没——我便诧异，自己当时竟没有抓起站在近旁的桌上放着的那支手枪，一枪把她当场了结，也算稍稍抵偿我给她造成的苦楚。我说如今想来诧异：那时我心里只想着硬撑到底。

「她朝我们走来，笔直如离弦之箭，脸上却带着那副神情。『我听得可对？』她唇间吐出的是这几个字。『您娶了我——如我此刻才明白的，一个身份低于您的女人——只因奉命如此？您不曾爱过我？不曾给过我那唯一使婚姻成为圣礼、甚至成为可能的东西？难道只因为我留在这屋里，您就必须离开这座由您垂死父亲的病躯圣化了的宅子？』

「我看见父亲僵硬灰白的嘴唇无声地翕动，仿佛想替我作答而不能；我便鼓起仅有的勇气告诉她，她听见了我不检点的话，我深以为憾。我说，无论我心里对那个挫败了我最亲爱、最珍视之希望的人怀着怎样的怨愤，我从来不曾有意伤害*她*。我说，我谦卑地求她恕宥，并且愿如此承认她对我的权利：倘若我的离去使她痛苦，我便答应此刻不离家门；我的用意不在伤害她，只在保全我自己。

「啊，听了这些软弱的话，她眉宇间升起的是怎样的轻蔑。感谢上帝，那不是对我的轻蔑——虽然那一时辰的我尽当得起——而是对我小看了她的轻蔑。

「『那么我听得不错。』她喃喃道，随即带着一副不容置辩的神情等待着。

「我只能低下头去，咒骂自己降生的那一日。
===Original===
“ ‘Holman! Holman!’ came in agonized entreaty from the bed, ‘you will not rob me of my daughter *now*?’

“Startled, I looked up. Luttra was halfway to the door.

“ ‘What are you going to do?’ cried I, bounding towards her.

“She stopped me with a look. ‘The son must never forsake the father,’ said she. ‘If either of us must leave the house this day, let it be I.’ Then in a softer tone, ‘When you asked me to be your wife, I who had worshipped you from the moment you entered my father’s house on the memorable night I left it, was so overcome at your condescension that I forgot you did not preface it by the usual passionate, “I love you,” which more than the marriage ring binds two hearts together. In the glamour and glow of my joy, I did not see that the smile that was in my heart, was missing from your face. I was to be your wife and that was enough, or so I thought then, for I loved you. Ah, and I do now, my husband, love you so that I leave you. Were it for your happiness I would do more than that, I would give you back your freedom, but from what I hear, it seems that you need a wife in name and I will be but fulfilling your desire in holding that place for you. I will never disgrace the position high as it is above my poor deserts. When the day comes﻿—if the day comes﻿—that you need or feel you need the sustainment of my presence or the devotion of my heart, no power on earth save that of death itself, shall keep me from your side. Till that day arrives I remain what you have made me, a bride who lays no claim to the name you this morning bestowed upon her.’ And with a gesture that was like a benediction, she turned, and noiselessly, breathlessly as a dream that vanishes, left the room.

“Sirs, I believe I uttered a cry and stumbled towards her. Someone in that room uttered a cry, but it may be that it only rose in my heart and that the one I heard came from my father’s lips. For when at the door I turned, startled at the deathly silence, I saw he had fainted on his pillow. I could not leave him so. Calling to Mrs. Daniels, who was never far from my father in those days, I bade her stop the lady﻿—I believe I called her my wife﻿—who was going down the stairs, and then rushed to his side. It took minutes to revive him. When he came to himself it was to ask for the creature who had flashed like a beacon of light upon his darkening path. I rose as if to fetch her but before I could advance I heard a voice say, ‘She is not here,’ and looking up I saw Mrs. Daniels glide into the room.

“ ‘Mrs. Blake has gone, sir, I could not keep her.’ ”

===Chinese===
「『霍尔曼！霍尔曼！』床上传来凄惶的哀告，『你总不至于*偏偏在这时候*把我的女儿夺走吧？』

「我一惊，抬头看去。卢特拉已走到通往房门的中途。

「『您要做什么？』我叫着，向她抢步过去。

「她用一个眼色止住了我。『儿子决不可离弃父亲。』她说，『今日若你我之中必有一人离开这座宅子，那就让我走吧。』随后她的声音柔和下来：『您要我做您的妻子之时——自从那个可纪念的夜晚、您走进我父亲家里的一刻起，我便一直敬慕着您——您肯屈尊相就，我感激得神魂无主，竟忘了您开口之前并没有先说出那句惯常的炽热的话，那句比结婚戒指更能把两颗心结在一处的话：「我爱你」。在欢欣的光焰与光彩里，我没有看出，我心里的那朵笑容，您的脸上并没有。我将成为您的妻子，这便够了——至少我当时是这样想，因为我爱您。啊，我如今也还爱您，我的丈夫，爱得甘愿离开您。倘若这样能成全您的幸福，我还可以做得更多，把您的自由还给您；可是照我听来的话，您似乎需要的只是一位有名分的妻子，那么我守住这个位置，正是遂您的心愿。这个名分纵然远在我微贱的身分之上，我也决不让它蒙受玷污。倘有一日——倘若真有一日——您需要、或自觉需要我在跟前扶持，需要我这颗心的忠爱，那么除了死亡本身，世上没有任何力量能拦住我不回到您身边。在那一天到来之前，我仍旧是您所造就的我：一个新娘，却不对您今晨赐予她的这个姓氏，索取任何权利。』说着，她作了一个近乎祝福的手势，转过身，像一场消散的梦，无声无息、屏息敛气地出了房间。

「诸位，我相信我叫了一声，踉跄着朝她奔去。那间屋里确有人叫了一声，可那声音也许只在我心里升起，我听见的那一声，或许出自父亲之口。因为在门口我为那死一般的寂静所惊，回头一看，他已晕倒在枕上。我不能撇下他不管。我唤来丹尼尔斯太太——那些日子里她从不远离我父亲身旁——叫她去拦住那位夫人——我记得我当时唤她作我的妻子——她正往楼下去；随后我抢到父亲身边。过了好几分钟他才缓过来。他一醒过来，便要找那个曾如灯塔一般照亮他暗路的身影。我起身似要去找她，还没迈步，便听一个声音说：『她不在这里。』抬头一看，丹尼尔斯太太已悄然走进房来。

「『布莱克太太走了，先生，我留不住她。』」

## XIII A Man’s Heart / 十三 一个男人的心

===Original===
“That was the last time my eyes ever rested upon my wife. Whither she went or what refuge she gained, I never knew. My father who had received in this scene a great shock, began to fail so rapidly, he demanded my constant care; and though from time to time as I ministered to him and noted with what a yearning persistency he would eye the door and then turn and meet my gaze with a look I could not understand, I caught myself asking whether I had done a deed destined to hang forever about me like a pall; it was not till after his death that the despairing image of the bright young creature to whom I had given my name, returned with any startling distinctness to my mind, or that I allowed myself to ask whether the heavy gloom which I now felt settling upon me was owing to the sense of shame that overpowered me at the remembrance of the past, or to the possible loss I had sustained in the departure of my young unloved bride.

“The announcement at this time of the engagement between Evelyn Blake and the Count De Mirac may have had something to do with this. Though I had never in the most passionate hours of my love for her, lost sight of that side of her nature which demanded as her right the luxury of great wealth; and though in my tacit abandonment of her and secret marriage with another I had certainly lost the right to complain of her actions whatever they might be, this manifest surrendering of herself to the power of wealth and show at the price of all that women are believed to hold dear, was an undoubted blow to my pride and the confidence I had till now unconsciously reposed in her inherent womanliness and affection. That she had but made on a more conspicuous scale, the same sacrifice as myself to the god of Wealth and Position, was in my eyes at that time, no palliation of her conduct. I was a man none too good or exalted at the best; she, a woman, should have been superior to the temptations that overpowered me. That she was not, seemed to drag all womanhood a little nearer the dust; fashionable womanhood I ought to say, for somehow even at that early day her conduct did not seem to affect the vivid image of Luttra standing upon my threshold, shorn of her joy but burning with a devotion I did not comprehend, and saying,

“ ‘I loved you. Ah, and I do yet, my husband, love you so that I leave you. When the day comes﻿—if the day comes﻿—you need or feel you need the sustainment of my presence or the devotion of my heart, no power on earth save that of death itself, shall keep me from your side.’

===Chinese===
“那是我最后一眼看见我的妻子。她去了哪里，投奔了什么容身之处，我始终不得而知。家父在这场面里受了大大的震动，衰败得极快，须我日夜侍奉；我虽在照料他的时候，不时察觉他总是那样渴念而不死心地盯着门口，然后转过脸来迎住我的目光，带着一种我读不懂的神情，因而暗自叩问，我做的事，是否注定要像一件尸衣似的永远罩在我身上；但直到他死后，那个我把姓氏给了她的、明媚年轻的人儿，那副绝望的形象，才带着惊人的清晰回到我心里，我才容许自己发问：如今沉沉压上我的这团阴郁，究竟是因为一想起往事便压倒我的羞愧，还是因为我那年轻而不曾爱过的新娘一去，我或许蒙受的损失。”

“这个当口传来的伊芙琳·布莱克与德·米拉克伯爵订婚的消息，或许同这有些干系。虽说我在爱她爱得最热烈的时辰，也不曾看不见她天性中那一边——把豪富的奢华当作自己当然的权利；虽说我在默然弃她、另娶他人的秘密婚姻里，早已失掉了抱怨她行动的资格，无论她做什么；可她这样公然把自己交给财富与排场的势力，代价是女人们素来被认为珍视的一切，对我无疑仍是一记打击——打击我的自尊，也打击我至今不自觉地寄托在她女性天性与情爱上的信赖。她不过是把我向财富与地位之神作的献祭，在一个更显眼的位置重作了一遍；这在当时的我眼里，丝毫不为她开脱。我本就是一个不怎么高尚的人；她是个女人，本该胜过那些压倒了我的诱惑。她竟不能，这似乎把整个女性都朝尘土拖近了一步——我该说，是把时髦的女性；因为不知怎么，即便在那时，她的行径也似乎无损于卢特拉立在我门槛上的那个鲜明的形象：她的欢乐被削夺了，却燃烧着一种我不懂的献身，她说：

“‘我爱过你。啊，我如今还爱，我的丈夫，爱你爱到我这才离开你。哪一天——若真有那一天——你需要、或觉得需要我在场的扶持、我这颗心的忠诚，那么除了死亡本身，世上没有任何力量能把我从你身边拉开。’

===Original===
“Yes, with the fading away of other faces and other forms, that face and that form now began to usurp the chief place in my thoughts. Not to my relief and pleasure. That could scarcely be, remembering all that had occurred; rather to my increasing distress and passionate resentment. I longed to forget I was held by a tie, that known to the world would cause me the bitterest shame. For by this time the true character of her father and brother had been revealed and I found myself bound to the daughter of a convicted criminal.

“But I could not forget *her*. The look with which she had left me was branded into my consciousness. Night and day it floated before me, till to escape it I resolved to fasten it upon canvas, if by that means I might succeed in eliminating it from my dreams.

“The painting you have seen this night is the result. Born with an artist’s touch and insight that under other circumstances might, perhaps, have raised me into the cold dry atmosphere of fame, the execution of this piece of work, presented but few difficulties to my somewhat accustomed hand. Day by day her beauty grew beneath my brush, startling me often with its spiritual force and significance till my mind grew feverish over its work, and I could scarcely refrain from rising at night to give a touch here or there to the floating golden hair or the piercing, tender eyes turned, ah, ever turned upon the inmost citadel of my heart with that look that slew my father before his time and made me, yes me, old in spirit even in the ardent years of my first manhood.

===Chinese===
“是的，随着别的面孔、别的身影渐渐褪去，那张脸、那个身影，如今开始篡据了我思念中的首席。这并非叫我宽慰欢喜。想到种种前情，那几乎不可能；倒是叫我愈发愁苦、愈发愤懑。我巴不得忘掉我被一根绳索拴着——这绳索一旦为世人所知，会带给我最苦的耻辱。因为到这时，她父亲和兄弟的真实面目已经败露，我发现自己竟系在一个判了罪的罪犯的女儿身上。

“可我忘不掉*她*。她离去时望着我的那一眼，烙进了我的意识。它日夜飘在我眼前，直到为了躲开它，我决意把它钉上画布，倘若借这个法子能把它从我的梦里清除出去。

“你们今夜见到的那幅画，便是结果。我生来带一点画家的笔触和眼力，在别的境遇里，或许足以把我抬进名誉那又冷又干的高空；这幅工作的完成，对我这不算生疏的手，没费多少难。一天一天，她的美在我的笔下跌露，往往以它灵性的力量和意味叫我吃惊，直到我的心智为这工作发起热来，夜里几乎按捺不住，想爬起来，往那飘拂的金发上、往那双锐利而温柔的眼睛上补一笔——那双眼睛，啊，永远转过来，望着我的心最深的堡垒，带着那一眼——那一眼叫我父亲未及天年而死，叫我，正是叫我，在人生最初的热烈年华里，精神已先老了。

===Original===
“At last it was finished and she stood before me lifelike and real in the very garments and with almost the very aspect of that never to be forgotten moment. Even the roses which in the secret uneasiness of my conscience I had put in her hand on our departure from Troy, as a sort of visible token that I regarded her as my bride, and which through all her interview with my father she had never dropped, blossomed before me on the canvas. Nothing that could give reality to the likeness, was lacking; the vision of my dreams stood embodied in my sight, and I looked for peace. Alas, that picture now became my dream.

“Inserting it behind that of Evelyn which for two years had held its place above my armchair, I turned its face to the wall when I rose in the morning. But at night it beamed ever upon me, becoming as the months passed, the one thing to hold to and muse over when the world grew a little noisy in my ears and the never ceasing conflict of the ages beat a trifle too loudly on heart and brain.

“Meanwhile no word of her, only of her villainous father and brother; no token that she had escaped evil or was removed from want. If I had loved her I could not have succored her, for I did not know where to find her. Her countenance illumined my wall, but her fair young self lay for all I knew sheltered within the darkness and silence of the tomb.

===Chinese===
“末了画成了，她立在我面前，栩栩如生，穿着正是那一身衣裳，几乎正是那个永不能忘的时刻的那副模样。连那几朵玫瑰也在画布上朝我开放——当年离开特洛伊时，我出于良心隐秘的不安，把它们放进她手里，当作我看她是我新娘的一个看得见的凭证；她在与我父亲那一场面对峙时，始终不曾让它们落地。凡能给这画像添一分真实的，一样不缺；我梦中的幻象活生生立在我眼前，我指望由此得些安宁。唉，那幅画如今倒成了我的梦。

“我把它插在伊芙琳那幅后面——那一幅在我的扶手椅上方挂了两年——每天早晨起身，便把它的脸转向墙。可一到夜里，它总照临着我；随着一个月一个月过去，当世界在我耳畔略略喧嚣起来、岁月那永不停歇的争斗在心头和脑际敲得稍重一点的时候，它成了我唯一可以抓住、可以对之沉思的东西。

“这期间没有她一个字的消息，只有她那恶徒父亲和兄弟的消息；没有一点凭证，说她躲过了邪恶，或免于匮乏。我若爱她，也无法接济她，因为我不知去何处寻她。她的面容照亮了我的墙，可她那年轻姣好的自身，据我所知，正安放在坟墓的黑暗与寂静里。

===Original===
“At length my morbid broodings worked out their natural result. A dull melancholy settled upon me which nothing could break. Even the news that my cousin who had lost her husband a month after marriage, had returned to America with expectation to remain, scarcely caused a ripple in my apathy. Was I sinking into a hypochrondriac? or was my passion for the beautiful brunette dead? I determined to solve the doubt.

“Seeking her where I knew she would be found, I gazed again upon her beauty. It was absolutely nothing to me. A fair young face with high thoughts in every glance floated like sunshine between us and I left the haughty Countess, with the knowledge burned deep into my brain, that the love I had considered slain was alive and demanding, but that the object of it past recall, was my lost young wife.

“Once assured of this, my apathy vanished like mist before a kindled torch. Henceforth the future held a hope, and life a purpose. I would seek my wife throughout the world and bring her back if I found her in prison between the men whose existence was a curse to my pride. But where should I turn my steps? What golden thread had she left in my hand by which to trace her through the labyrinth of this world? I could think of but one, and that was the love which would restrain her from going away from me too far. The Luttra of old would not leave the city where her husband lived. If she was not changed, I ought to be able to find her somewhere within this great Babylon of ours. Wisdom told me to set the police upon her track, but pride bade me try every other means first. So with the feverish energy of one leading a forlorn hope, I began to pace the streets if haply I might see her face shine upon me from the crowd of passers by; a foolish fancy, unproductive of result! I not only failed to see her, but anyone like her.

===Chinese===
“终于，我这病态的忧思结出了它自然的结果。一种打不破的沉沉忧郁罩住了我。连我表妹的消息——她婚后一个月便死了丈夫，如今回美国来，打算长住——也几乎不能在我这片冷淡里激起一丝涟漪。我是在沉入疑病症么？还是我对那黑发美人的热情已经死了？我决意解开这个疑团。

“我到我知道能寻着她的地方去找她，重新凝望她的美貌。它于我竟然什么也不是了。一张明媚年轻的脸，每一瞥里都含着高洁的思想，像阳光一般浮在我们中间；我离开那位倨傲的伯爵夫人时，有一个认识深深烧进我的脑子：我原以为已死的那份爱，还活着，还在索求；只是它那无可挽回的对象，是我那失去的年轻妻子。

“一经确信这一点，我的冷淡便像点起的火把前的雾一样消散了。从此，将来有了一个希望，人生有了一个目的。我要走遍天下寻我的妻子，哪怕发现她被关在监狱里，夹在那两个以其存在为我的自尊之辱的人中间，也要把她带回来。可是我该把脚步转向哪里？她在我的手里留下过什么金线，好让我循着它穿过这人世的迷宫？我只能想起一条，便是那不容她离我太远的爱情。旧日的卢特拉，不会离开她丈夫居住的城市。她若没有变，我便该能在我们这座大巴比伦的某个角落找到她。理智叫我把警察放上她的踪迹，骄傲却叫我先试尽别的法子。于是我带着领头攻打无望之阵的人的那股狂热，开始奔走一条一条街道，指望或许能从过往人群里看见她的脸朝我放光；一个愚蠢的痴想，一无所获！我不但没有看见她，连一个像她的人也没看见。

===Original===
“In the midst of the despair occasioned by this failure a thought flashed across me or rather a remembrance. One night not long since, being uncommonly restless, I had risen from my bed, dressed me and gone out into the yard back of my house for a little air. It was an unusual thing for me to do but I seemed to be suffocating where I was, and nothing else would satisfy me. As you already surmise, it was the night on which disappeared the sewing girl of which you have so often spoken, but I knew nothing of that, my thoughts were far from my own home and its concerns. You may judge what a state of mind I was in when I tell you that I even thought at one moment while I paused before the gate leading into —﻿—﻿— Street that I saw the face of her with whom my thoughts were ever busy, peering upon me through the bars.

“You tell me that I did see a girl there, and that it was the one who had lived as sewing woman in my house; it may be so, but at the time I considered it a vision of my wife, and the remembrance of it, coming as it did after my repeated failures to encounter her in the street, worked a change in my plans. For regard it as weakness or not, the recollection that the vision I had seen wore the garments of a working-woman rather than a lady, acted upon me like a warning not to search for her any longer among the resorts of the well-dressed, but in the regions of poverty and toil. I therefore took to wanderings such as I have no heart to describe. Nor do I need to, if, as you have informed me, I have been followed.

“The result was almost madness. Though deep in my heart I felt a steadfast trust in the purity of her intentions, the fear of what she might have been driven to by the awful poverty and despair I every day saw seething about me, was like hot steel in brain and heart. Then her father and her brother! To what might they not have forced her, innocent and loving soul though she was! Drinking the dregs of a cup such as I had never considered it possible for me to taste, I got so far as to believe that her eyes would yet flash upon me from beneath some of the tattered shawls I saw sullying the forms of the young girls upon which I hourly stumbled. Yes, and even made a move to see my cousin, if haply I could so win upon her compassion as to gain her consent to shelter the poor creature of my dreams in case the necessity came. But my heart failed me at the sight of her cold face above the splendor she had bought with her charms, and I was saved a humiliation I might never have risen above.

===Chinese===
“在这失败带来的绝望中间，一个念头——不如说一段回忆——闪过我。不久前的一个夜里，我心神异常不宁，从床上爬起来，穿好衣裳，走到宅子后面的院子里去透口气。这在我是不常有的事，可我待在原处像是就要窒息，非这样不可。正如你们已经猜到的，那就是你们常常提起的那个针线姑娘失踪的一夜；可我当时一无所知，我的心思远在我自己的家和家事之外。我告诉你们，当我立在通往——街的那道栅门前，有一瞬间竟以为我看见了我心头萦绕的那个人的脸，正隔着栅栏窥视我——你们可想而知我当时是怎样一种心境。

“你们告诉我，我当真在那里看见过一个姑娘，而且就是在我家做过针线妇的那一个；也许是这样；可在当时，我只当它是我妻子的一次显形，而这回忆——它在我在街上屡屡寻她不遇之后到来——改变了我的计划。因为，不管这算不算软弱，我记得那幻影穿着的是一个劳作妇人的衣裳，而不是贵妇的衣裳，这记忆对我像一声警告：不要再到衣冠楚楚之辈的去处寻她，要到穷苦与劳作的地界去寻。于是我开始了我不忍描摹的游荡。也不必描摹，倘若如你们所告知的，我一直被人跟着。

“其结果几乎是疯狂。虽说我心底对她心志的纯洁存着一种不动摇的信任，可是一想到她也许已被我天天看见在四周沸腾的那种可怕的贫穷与绝望逼到什么地步，那恐惧便像烧红的钢，烙在脑里心里。还有她的父亲和她的兄弟！他们还有什么不会强迫她去做的，尽管她是个又天真又多情的魂灵！我喝着我从不认为自己可能尝到的杯底的苦酒，竟至相信，我时时撞见的那些糟蹋在年轻姑娘身上的破烂披肩底下，会有一双她的眼睛朝我闪出光来。是的，我甚至动了念头要去见我表妹，指望能如此赢得她的怜悯，使她答应在必要的时候，收容我梦中的那个可怜人。可是一见她那张冷脸，高踞在她以色相换来的一片富丽之上，我的心又怯了，这才免了一场我或许永远爬不起来的羞辱。

===Original===
“At last, one day I saw a girl﻿—no, it was not she, but her hair was similar to hers in hue, and the impulse to follow her was irresistible. I did more than that, I spoke to her. I asked her if she could tell me anything of one whose locks were golden red like hers﻿—But I need not tell you what I said nor what she replied with a gentle delicacy that was almost a shock to me as showing from what heights to what depths a woman can fall. Enough that nothing passed between us beyond what I have intimated, and that in all she said she gave me no news of Luttra.

“Next day I started for the rambling old house in Vermont, if haply in the spot where I first saw her, I might come upon some clue to her present whereabouts. But the old inn was deserted, and whatever hope I may have had in that direction, perished with the rest.

“Concerning the contents of that bureau-drawer above, I can say nothing. If, as I scarcely dare to hope, they should prove to have been indeed brought here by the girl who has since disappeared so strangely, who knows but what in those folded garments a clue is given which will lead me at last to the knowledge for which I would now barter all I possess. My wife﻿—But I can mention her name no more till the question that now assails us is set at rest. Mrs. Daniels must﻿—”

But at that moment the door opened and Mrs. Daniels came in.

===Chinese===
“后来有一天，我看见一个姑娘——不，那不是她，可她的头发同她的颜色相似，跟上去的冲动叫我无法抵抗。我不止跟了，我还同她说话。我问她，可知道一个头发同她一样金红的人的消息——但我何必告诉你们我说了什么，她又怎样以一种温柔的斯文回答我；那份斯文几乎叫我一惊，因为它显出一个女人能从怎样的高处跌到怎样的深处。只消说，我们之间没有发生任何超出我方才暗示之外的事，她所说的一切里，也没有一句给我带来卢特拉的音信。

“第二天我便动身去佛蒙特那座散乱的老宅，指望在我头一回见到她的地方，或许能撞见一点她如今下落的线索。可那家老客栈空无人迹，我朝那个方向或许抱过的一切指望，也同其余的一并破灭了。

“至于楼上那口五斗柜抽屉里的东西，我无可奉告。倘若——我几乎不敢这样指望——它们当真是后来失踪得那般离奇的姑娘带到这里来的，谁知道呢，说不定那几件叠着的衣裳里，就藏着一条线索，最终引我得到那个我如今愿拿全部所有去换的答案。我的妻子——可眼下这个纠缠我们的问题没有个着落之前，我再不能提她的名字了。丹尼尔斯太太必须——”

可就在那时，门开了，丹尼尔斯太太走了进来。

## XIV Mrs. Daniels / 十四 丹尼尔斯太太

===Original===
She still wore her bonnet and shawl and her face was like marble.

“You want me?” said she with a hurried look towards Mr. Blake that had as much fear as surprise in it.

“Yes,” murmured that gentleman moving towards her with an effort we could very well appreciate. “Mrs. Daniels, who was the girl you harbored in that room above us for so long? Speak; what was her name and where did she come from?”

The housekeeper trembling in every limb, cast us one hurried appeal.

===Chinese===
她还戴着帽子、披着披肩，脸白得像大理石。

“您要我？”她说，朝布莱克先生匆匆投去一瞥，那里面有惊讶，也有同样多的惧意。

“是的，”那位先生低声道，朝她走近，那种吃力，我们非常能够体会。“丹尼尔斯太太，你在这楼上那间屋里藏了那么久的，是谁家的姑娘？说吧；她叫什么名字，从哪里来？”

管家四肢发着抖，朝我们投来匆促求告的一眼。

===Original===
“Speak!” reechoed Mr. Gryce; “the time for secrecy has passed.”

“O,” cried she, sinking into a chair from sheer inability to stand, “it was your wife, Mr. Blake, the young creature you﻿—”

“Ah!”

All the agony, the hopelessness, the love, the passion of those last few months flashed up in that word. She stopped as if she had been shot, but seeing the hand which he had hurriedly raised, fall slowly before him, went on with a burst,

===Chinese===
“说吧！”格赖斯先生重复道；“守秘密的时辰过去了。”

“哦，”她喊道，双腿再支撑不住，跌坐在椅子里，“那是您的妻子啊，布莱克先生，那个年轻的、被您——”

“啊！”

那几个月的全部苦痛、无望、爱与激情，都在这一个字里迸发了出来。她像中了枪似的顿住；但见他仓皇举起的那只手在他面前缓缓垂落，便又一口气说了下去：

===Original===
“O sir, she made me swear on my knees I would never betray her, no matter what happened. When not two weeks after your father died she came to the house and asking for me, told me all her story and all her love; how she could not reconcile it with her idea of a wife’s duty to live under any other roof than that of her husband, and lifting off the black wig which she wore, showed me how altered she had made herself by that simple change﻿—in her case more marked by the fact that her eyes were in keeping with black hair, while with her own bright locks they always gave you a shock as of something strange and haunting﻿—I gave up my will as if forced by a magnetic power, and not only opened the house to her but my heart as well; swearing to all she demanded and keeping my oath too, as I would preserve my soul from sin and my life from the knife of the destroyer.”

“But, when she went,” broke from the pallid lips of the man before her, “when she was taken away from the house, what then?”

“Ah,” returned the agitated woman, “what then! Do you not think I suffered? To be held by my oath, an oath I was satisfied she would wish kept even at this crisis, yet knowing all the while she was drifting away into some evil that you, if you knew who she was, would give your life to avert from your honor if not from her innocent head! To see you cold, indifferent, absorbed in other things, while she, who would have perished any day for your happiness, was losing her life perhaps in the clutches of those horrible villains! Do not ask me to tell you what I have suffered since she went; I can never tell you﻿—innocent, tender, noble-hearted creature that she was.”

===Chinese===
“哦先生，她叫我跪着起誓，无论出什么事，永不泄露她。您父亲死后不到两个星期，她来到宅里，指名找我，把她的身世和她的爱统统告诉了我；说她如何不能把她对妻子本分的想法，调和到住进丈夫以外任何屋顶下的地步；说着摘掉她戴的黑假发，给我看这简单的一换，她把自己变到了什么田地——在她尤其惹眼，因为她的眼睛本衬黑发，一旦配上她自己那头明亮的头发，那双眼睛总给你一种古怪而萦怀的震骇——我的意志缴了械，仿佛被一股磁力强夺了去；我不但向她开了宅门，也向她开了我的心门；她要求什么我起誓什么，且一直守着誓言，就像我要保全我的灵魂不堕罪恶、保全我的性命不落刀口一样。”

“可是她走的时候，”从眼前这人灰白的嘴唇里迸出来，“她被人从宅里带走的时候，又怎样了呢？”

“唉，”激动的妇人答道，“又怎样呢！您以为我不曾受煎熬么？被誓言捆着——我深信即便到这个关头，她也但愿我守住它——却时时刻刻明知她正漂进某种祸事里；那祸事，您若知道她是谁，会不惜性命去挡开的——若不为她无辜的头颅，也为您的名誉！眼看着您冷淡、漠然、专心在别的事情上，而她，那个为了您的幸福哪一天都甘愿毁掉自己的人，也许正在那两个可怕恶徒的魔掌里送命！别叫我告诉您，她走后我受了什么罪；我永远说不出口——她那么天真、那么温柔、那么一副高贵心肠的人儿。”

===Original===
“Was?” His hand clutched his heart as if it had been seized by a deathly spasm. “Why do you say was?”

“Because I have just come from the Morgue where she lies dead.”

“No, no,” came in a low shriek from his lips, “that is not she; that is another woman, like her perhaps, but not she.”

===Chinese===
“‘曾经是’？”他的手抓住自己的胸口，像被一阵致命的痉挛攫住。“你为什么说‘曾经是’？”

因为我刚从陈尸所来，她躺在那里，死了。

“不，不，”他唇间发出一声低低的哀号，“那不是她；那是另一个女人，也许像她，可不是她。”

===Original===
“Would to God you were right; but the long golden braids! Such hair as hers I never saw on anyone before.”

“Mr. Blake is right,” I broke in, for I could not endure this scene any longer. “The woman taken out of the East river today has been both seen and spoken to by him and that not long since. He should know if it is his wife.”

“And isn’t it?”

===Chinese===
“但愿上帝让您说中了吧；可是那长长的金发辫！那样的头发，我从来没有在别人头上见过。”

“布莱克先生说得对，”我插嘴道，因为这场面我实在看不下去了。“今天从东河里捞起来的那个女人，他见过，也同她说过话，而且就在不久之前。她是不是他的妻子，他应该知道。”

“那么不是她？”

===Original===
“No, a thousand times no; the girl was a perfect stranger.”

The assurance seemed to lift a leaden weight from her heart. “O thank God,” she murmured dropping with an irresistible impulse on her knees. Then with a sudden return of her old tremble, “But I was only to reveal her secret in case of her death! What have I done, O what have I done! Her only hope lay in my faithfulness.”

Mr. Blake leaning heavily on the table before him, looked in her face.

===Chinese===
“不是，一千个一万个不是；那姑娘完全是个陌生人。”

这句保证仿佛从她心上卸下了一块铅。“哦，感谢上帝，”她喃喃着，情不自禁地跪了下去。随后又忽然抖起来，像先前那样：“可是我原本只该在她死了的时候，才泄露她的秘密啊！我做了什么，哦，我做了什么！她唯一的指望，原在我的一片忠心上。”

布莱克先生沉沉地倚着面前的桌子，望着她的脸。

===Original===
“Mrs. Daniels,” said he, “I love my wife; her hope now lies in me.”

She leaped to her feet with a joyous bound. “You love her? O thank God!” she again reiterated but this time in a low murmur to her self. “Thank God!” and weeping with unrestrained joy, she drew back into a corner.

Of course after that, all that remained for us to do was to lay our heads together and consult as to the best method of renewing our search after the unhappy girl, now rendered of double interest to us by the facts with which we had just been made acquainted. That she had been forced away from the roof that sheltered her by the power of her father and brother was of course no longer open to doubt. To discover them, therefore, meant to recover her. Do you wonder, then, that from the moment we left Mr. Blake’s house, the capture of that brace of thieves became the leading purpose of our two lives?

===Chinese===
“丹尼尔斯太太，”他说，“我爱我的妻子；如今她的指望在我身上。”

她欢欣得一跃而起。“您爱她？哦，感谢上帝！”她又说了一遍，这一次是低低的自语。“感谢上帝！”随即喜极而泣，也不收敛，退到一个角落里去了。

事情到了这个地步，我们余下能做的，自然只有凑在一处商议，看用什么法子重新搜寻那不幸的姑娘最好——经方才知道的这些情由，她于我们已有了双重的分量。她是被她父亲和兄弟的势力，从庇护她的屋顶下强行架走的，这自然再无可疑。因此，寻见他们，就等于寻回她。那么，从我们离开布莱克先生宅邸的那一刻起，缉拿那一对窃贼成了我们两人毕生的头等目标，您还会奇怪么？

## XV A Confab / 十五 一席密谈

===Original===
Next morning Mr. Gryce and I met in serious consultation. How, and in what direction should we extend the inquiries necessary to a discovery of these Schoenmakers?

“I advise a thorough overhauling of the German quarter,” said my superior. “Schmidt, and Rosenthal will help us and the result ought to be satisfactory.”

But I shook my head at this. “I don’t believe,” said I, “that they will hide among their own people. You must remember they are not alone, but have with them a young woman of a somewhat distinguished appearance, whose presence in a crowded district, like that, would be sure to awaken gossip; something which above all else they must want to avoid.”

“That is true; the Germans are a dreadful race for gossip.”

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，格赖斯先生同我碰头，郑重商议。要寻出这舍恩马克父子，我们的查访该怎样进行、朝哪个方向进行？

“我主张把德国人聚居区彻底翻检一遍，”我的上司说。“施密特和罗森塔尔会帮我们，结果应该叫人满意。”

对此我却摇头。“我不信，”我说，“他们会藏在本族人中间。您得记得，他们不是孤身两人，身边还带着一个模样相当出众的年轻女人；她这样的人一出现在那样人烟稠密的地段，必定惹出闲话——这闲话，正是他们比什么都唯恐避之不及的。”

“这话不假；德国人这个族类，闲话起来可了不得。”

===Original===
“If they dared to ill-dress her or ill-treat her, it would be different. But she is a valuable piece of property to them you see, a choice lot of goods which it is for their interest to preserve in first-class condition till the day comes for its disposal. For I presume you have no doubt that it is for the purpose of extorting money from Mr. Blake that they have carried off his young wife.”

“For that reason or one similar. He is a man of resources, they may have hoped he would help them to escape the country.”

“If they don’t hide in the German quarter they certainly won’t in the Italian, French or Irish. What they want is too keep close and rouse no questions. I think they will be found to have gone up the river somewhere, or over to Jersey. Hoboken wouldn’t be a bad place to send Schmidt to.”

===Chinese===
“倘若他们胆敢叫她穿得寒酸，或是虐待她，那就另当别论。可您瞧，她于他们是一件值钱的货色，一批上等的货，值得他们保管得妥妥帖帖，直到脱手的日子。因为我料想，您不会怀疑：他们拐走他这位年轻的妻子，正为的是从布莱克先生身上榨钱。”

“为这个缘故，或差不多的缘故。他是个有财有路数的人，他们也许指望他助他们逃出这个国家。”

“他们若不藏德国人聚居区，便断然不会藏意大利人、法国人或爱尔兰人的地段。他们要的是躲得严实，不惹人发问。我想，到头来会发现他们是在河上游的什么去处，要么就过了河到泽西。派施密特去霍博肯，倒不失为一步好棋。”

===Original===
“You forget what it is they’ve got on their minds; besides no conspicuous party such as they could live in a rural district without attracting more attention than in the most crowded tenement house in the city.”

“Where do you think, then, they would be liable to go?”

“Well my most matured thought on the subject,” returned Mr. Gryce, after a moment’s deliberation, “is this﻿—you say, and I agree, that they have hampered themselves with this woman at this time for the purpose of using her hereafter in a scheme of blackmail upon Mr. Blake. He, then, must be the object about which their thoughts revolve and toward which whatever operations or plans they may be engaged upon must tend. What follows? When a company of men have made up their minds to rob a bank, what is the first thing they do? They hire, if possible, a house next to the especial building they intend to enter, and for months work upon the secret passage through which they hope to reach the safe and its contents; or they make friends with the watchman that guards its treasures, and the janitor who opens and shuts the doors. In short they hang about their prey before they pounce upon it. And so will these Schoenmakers do in the somewhat different robbery which they plan sooner or later to effect. Whatever may keep them close at this moment, Mr. Blake and Mr. Blake’s house is the point toward which their eyes are turned, and if we had time﻿—”

===Chinese===
“您忘了他们心里压着的是什么事；再说，像他们这样惹眼的一伙，住在乡间地带，比住在城里最挤的贫民公寓里还更招眼。”

“那么依你看，他们会往哪里去呢？”

“唔，我在这事上想得最成熟的一个念头，”格赖斯先生沉吟片刻后答道，“是这样——你说，我也同意，他们这会儿拖上这个女人，为的是日后拿她在布莱克先生身上施一桩敲诈。那么，他必是他们心思旋转的轴心，他们的一切动作与计划，无论正在进行什么，都必朝他汇聚。由此推出什么？一伙人打定了主意要抢银行，头一件事做什么？可能的话，他们租下紧挨着目标的那所房子，一连几个月开凿秘密通道，指望由它通到保险柜和柜中之物；要么就同看守财宝的更夫、开门闭户的司阍交朋友。总之，扑上去之前，先在猎物近旁徘徊。这两个舍恩马克，在他们迟早要实施的这场不大一样的抢劫里，也会如法炮制。此刻无论是什么把他们拴在近处，布莱克先生和布莱克先生的宅邸，终是他们眼睛盯住的那一点，倘若我们有工夫——”

===Original===
“But we haven’t,” I broke in impetuously. “It is horrible to think of that grand woman languishing away in the power of such rascals.”

“If we had time,” Mr. Gryce persisted, “all it would be necessary to do would be to wait, they would come into our hands as easily and naturally as a hawk into the snare of the fowler. But as you say we have not, and therefore, I would recommend a little beating of the bush directly about Mr. Blake’s house; for if all my experience is not at fault, those men are already within eyeshot of the prey they intend to run down.”

“But,” said I, “I have been living myself in that very neighborhood and know by this time the ways of every house in the vicinity. There is not a spot up and down the Avenue for ten blocks where they could hide away for two days much less two weeks. And as for the side streets﻿—why I could tell you the names of those who live in each house for a considerable distance. Yet if you say so I will go to work﻿—”

===Chinese===
“可我们没有，”我按捺不住，插嘴道。“一想到那样一位雍容的女人，萎顿在这样一伙恶徒手里，就叫人不寒而栗。”

“倘若我们有工夫，”格赖斯先生执意说下去，“那么只消守候，他们自会像老鹰落进捕鹰人的罗网一样，又轻易又自然地落进我们手里。可是正如您说的，我们没有工夫；因此我建议，就在布莱克先生宅子四近，直接做一番打草惊蛇。因为倘若我的全部经验没有弄错，那两个人早已蹲在他们打算扑倒的猎物的视线之内了。”

“可是，”我说，“我自己就在那一带住着，到如今，左近每一户的底细我都摸熟了。这条大道上下十个街区，没有一个角落容得他们藏上两天，更别说两个星期。至于横街——我简直可以把相当一段路上每户人家的姓名一一说给您听。不过您若这样说，我就去着手——”

===Original===
“Do, and meanwhile Schmidt and Rosenthal shall rummage the German quarter and even go through Williamsburgh and Hoboken. The end justifies any amount of labor that can be spent upon this matter.”

“And you,” I asked.

“Will do my part when you have done yours.”

===Chinese===
“去办吧；同时叫施密特和罗森塔尔去搜德国人聚居区，连威廉斯堡和霍博肯也走一遍。为了这个案子，费多少力气都值。”

“那么您呢？”我问。

“你们做完了你们那份，我自然做我这份。”

## XVI The Mark of the Red Cross / 十六 红十字记号

===Original===
And what success did I meet? The best in the world. And by what means did I attain it? By that of the simplest, prettiest clue I ever came upon. But let me explain.

When after a wearisome day spent in an ineffectual search through the neighborhood, I went home to my room, which as you remember was a front one in a lodging-house on the opposite corner from Mr. Blake, I was so absorbed in mind and perhaps I may say shaken in nerve, by the strain under which I had been laboring for some time now, that I stumbled up an extra flight of stairs, and without any suspicion of the fact, tried the door of the room directly over mine. It is a wonder to me now that I could have made the mistake, for the halls were totally dissimilar, the one above being much more cut up than the one below, besides being flanked by a greater number of doors. But the intoxication of the mind is not far removed from that of the body, and as I say it was not till I had tried the door and found it locked, that I became aware of the mistake I had made.

With the foolish sense of shame that always overcomes us at the committal of any such trivial error, I stumbled hastily back, when my foot trod upon something that broke under my weight. I never let even small things pass without some notice. Stooping, then, for what I had thus inadvertently crushed, I carried it to where a single gas jet turned down very low, made a partial light in the long hall, and examining it, found it to be a piece of red chalk.

What was there in that simple fact to make me start and hastily recall one or two half-forgotten incidents which, once brought to mind, awoke a train of thought that led to the discovery and capture of those two desperate thieves? I will tell you.

I don’t remember now whether in my account of the visit I paid to the Schoenmakers’ house in Vermont, I informed you of the red cross I noticed scrawled on the panel of one of the doors. It seemed a trivial thing at the time and made little or no impression upon me, the chances being that I should never have thought of it again, if I had not come upon the article just mentioned at a moment when my mind was full of those very Schoenmakers. But remembered now, together with another half-forgotten fact﻿—that some days previous I had been told by the woman who kept the house I was in, that the parties over my head (two men and a woman I believe she said) were giving her some trouble, but that they paid well and therefore she did not like to turn them out﻿—it aroused a vague suspicion in my mind, and led to my walking back to the door I had endeavored to open in my abstraction, and carefully looking at it.

===Chinese===
那么，我收获了怎样的成功呢？天底下再好没有的成功。又是凭了什么手段得来的？凭的是我平生所遇最简单、最漂亮的一条线索。且听我细细道来。

那一天，我在左近寻访了整整一日，一无所获，拖着疲乏的身子回到寓所。您总记得，我的屋子在一家寄宿公寓的临街一面，恰与布莱克先生宅子所在的街角遥遥斜对。连日劳心，我此刻神思恍惚，或者还可以说心神震颤，竟多上了一层楼梯，浑然不觉，伸手去开我那间屋子正上方的一扇房门。如今想来，我竟会弄出这样的错处，真是奇事：两层的楼道全然不同，上头那层比下头分隔得更碎，旁侧的门也更多。可是心神的沉醉与身体的醉酒原相去不远；正如我方才所说，直到我拧了拧那门、发现它上了锁，我才醒悟自己走错了。

人一犯这类小小的错处，总要生出一股傻气的羞愧，我也不能免；正踉跄着急忙退回，脚下却踩着一样东西，被我的分量压碎了。再小的东西，我也从不肯轻易放过。于是我俯身拾起那无意间踩碎的东西，拿到长廊里那盏拧得极低、只漏出一点光亮的煤气灯下，仔细一验，原来是段红粉笔。

这么简单的一件事，里头到底有什么，竟能叫我悚然一惊，急急记起一两桩早已淡忘的旧事；而这几桩事一旦回到心头，又牵出一串思绪，直通到那两个亡命窃贼的查获与就擒？我这就讲给您听。

我如今记不清了：先前讲述我去佛蒙特舍恩马克家造访的那一段时，可曾告诉过您，我曾在他们一扇门的门板上，注意到一个用笔涂画的红十字。当时这似乎是桩小事，在我心里几乎没留下什么印象；若不是恰在满脑子都是舍恩马克一家的时候撞见了方才那段粉笔，我多半一辈子再想不起它来。可是此刻它连同另一桩淡忘的事实——几天前，我寓所的女主人曾告诉我，说住在我头顶上的那几口人（我记得她说的是两个男人和一个姑娘）颇叫她头疼，只是他们给钱爽快，她不忍撵他们走——一起想了起来，便在我心头唤起一团模糊的疑云，引着我走回方才恍恍惚惚想去开的那扇门前，细细端详。

===Original===
It was plain and white, rather ruder of make than those below, but offering no inducements for prolonged scrutiny. But not so with the one that stood at right angles to it on the left. Full in the centre of that, I beheld distinctly scrawled, probably with the very piece of chalk I then held, a red cross precisely similar in outline to the one I had seen a few days before on the panel of the Schoenmakers’ door at Granby.

The discovery sent a thrill over me that almost raised my hair on end. Was, then, this famous trio to be found in the very house in which I had been myself living for a week or more? over my head in fact? I could not withdraw my gaze from the mysterious looking object. I bent near, I listened, I heard what sounded like the suppressed snore of a powerful man, and almost had to lay hold of myself to prevent my hand from pushing open that closed door and my feet from entering. As it was I did finger the knob a little, but an extra loud snore from within reminded me by its suggestion of strength that I was but a small man and that in this case and at this hour, discretion was the better part of valor.

I therefore withdrew, but for the whole night lay awake listening to catch any sounds that might come from above, and going so far as to plan what I would do if it should be proved that I was indeed upon the trail of the men I was so anxious to encounter.

With the breaking of day I was upon my feet. A rude step had gone up the stairs a few minutes before and I was all alert to follow. But I presently considered that my wisest course would be to sound the landlady and learn if possible with what sort of characters I had to deal. Routing her out of the kitchen, where at that early hour she was already engaged in domestic duties, I drew her into a retired corner and put my questions. She was not backward in replying. She had conceived an innocent liking for me in the short time I had been with her﻿—a display of weakness for which I was myself, perhaps, as much to blame as she﻿—and was only too ready to pour out her griefs into my sympathizing ear. For those men were a grief to her, acceptable as was the money they were careful to provide her with. They were not only always in the house, that is one of them, smoking his old pipe and blackening up the walls, but they looked so shabby, and kept the girl so close, and if they did go out, came in at such unheard of hours. It was enough to drive her crazy; yet the money, the *money*﻿—

“Yes,” said I, “I know; and the money ought to make you overlook all the small disagreeablenesses you mention. What is a landlady without patience.” And I urged her not to turn them out.

===Chinese===
那门素白无饰，做工比楼下的还粗糙些，本不值得久看。可它左手成直角相邻的那扇门就不同了。那扇门的正中央，分明涂画着一个红十字——多半就是用我手中这段粉笔画的——轮廓同我几天前在格兰比舍恩马克家门板上见到的那个，一模一样。

这一发现叫我浑身一麻，头发几乎倒竖起来。莫非，这赫赫有名的三人行，就藏在我自己住了一个多星期的这座寓所里？就住在我头顶上？我的目光离不开那神秘的东西。我俯身过去，侧耳细听，听到一种像是壮汉被压住的鼾声，几乎得狠狠按住自己，才没有一掌推开那扇闭着的门、一脚跨进去。话虽如此，我的手指毕竟还是碰了碰门钮；屋里随即响起一声格外沉亮的鼾，那鼾声里透出的力气提醒我：我不过是个小个子，此刻此境，谨慎才是勇气的上策。

于是我退了回来。可整整一夜我睁眼躺着，捕捉头顶上可能落下的任何声息，甚至盘算起来：倘若当真证明我追的正是我急欲会一会的这两个人，我该怎么办。

天方破晓我便下了床。几分钟前有一阵沉重的脚步上了楼，我浑身警醒，正要跟上；转念一想，最稳妥的办法还是先去探探房东太太的口风，弄清楚我打交道的是什么路数的人物。清晨时分她已在厨房里忙起家务，我把她从厨房里请出来，引到一个僻静角落，问了我的话。她回答起来毫无遮拦。我住她这里的短短日子，她已对我生出一片天真的好感——这份软弱，我自己恐怕也得担一半不是——正巴不得把她的一肚子苦水倒进我这只同情的耳朵。原来那两个男人在她就是一桩苦恼，尽管他们仔细供给的钱财颇可人心。他们不光成天守在屋里——总有那么一个，叼着他的旧烟斗，把墙壁熏得乌黑——而且模样寒酸，又把那姑娘拘得那么紧；偶或出门，回来的时辰又荒唐得闻所未闻。这足够把她逼疯；可是那钱，那*钱*——

「是了，」我说，「我明白；有这钱，您就该把您说的这些小小不痛快都看开才是。做房东太太的，没有耐性还成什么。」我劝她千万莫撵他们走。

===Original===
“But the girl,” she went on, “so nice, so quiet, so sick-looking! I cannot stand it to see her cooped up in that small room, always watched over by one or both of those burly wretches. The old man says she is his daughter and she does not deny it, but I would as soon think of that little rosy child you see cooing in the window over the way, belonging to the beggar going in at the gate, as of her with her ladylike ways having any connection with him and his rough-acting son. You ought to see her﻿—”

“That is just what I want to do,” interrupted I. “Not because you have tempted my fancy by a recital of her charms,” I hastened to add, “but because she is, if I don’t mistake, a woman for whose discovery and rescue, a large sum of money has been offered.”

And without further disguise I acquainted the startled woman before me with the fact that I was not, as she had always considered, the clerk out of employment whose daily business it was to sally forth in quest of a situation, but a member of the city police.

She was duly impressed and easily persuaded to second all my operations as far as her poor wits would allow, giving me free range of her upper story, and above all, promising that secrecy without which all my finely laid plans for capturing the rogues without raising a scandal, would fall headlong to the ground.

Behold me, then, by noon of that same day domiciled in an apartment next to the one whose door bore that scarlet sign which had aroused within me such feverish hopes the night before. Clad in the seedy garments of a broken down French artist whose acquaintance I had once made, with something of his air and general appearance and with a few of his wretched daubs hung about on the whitewashed wall, I commenced with every prospect of success as I thought, that quiet espionage of the hall and its inhabitants which I considered necessary to a proper attainment of the end I had in view.

===Chinese===
「可那姑娘，」她接着说，「那样好，那样安静，那副病容！眼见她关在那间小屋里，日日夜夜由那两个恶棍中的一个或两个守着，我实在看不下去。老头子说她是他的女儿，她也不否认；可要我信她那副大家闺秀的气派同他和他那粗野的儿子有什么干系，还不如叫我相信街对面窗口里咿呀逗人的那个红喷喷的小娃娃，是正进门去的那个叫花子的种呢。您真该去看看她——」

「这正是我要做的。」我插嘴道。「倒不是因为您把她的好处数说得动了我的心，」我忙着补上一句，「而是因为，倘若我没有弄错，她是一位悬了重赏寻访搭救的女子。」

我也不再遮掩，径直向眼前这位大吃一惊的太太挑明：我并非她一向认定的那个失了业、每日出门寻差事的文书，而是本市警察中的一员。

她应景地肃然起敬，也容易就说服了，答应在她那点可怜的智力所及之处，襄助我的一切布置：楼上任我自由出入，尤其是许诺守口如瓶——没有这份保密，我为不动声色拿下那两个恶棍而精心布置的一切计划，都要一头栽到地上，摔个粉碎。

于是，当天正午，您瞧，我已住进了紧邻那扇门的一间屋子——门上那个猩红的记号，头天夜里曾在我心头燃起那样滚烫的希望。我裹上一身破旧衣裳，扮的是我从前结识的一个落魄法国画家的行头，学了他几分神气和做派，又在粉刷的墙上挂了几幅他的蹩脚涂鸦。我以为万无一失，便着手进行那桩安静的侦查——盯住楼道和楼道里的住户；要妥妥当当达到我的目的，这一步是少不得的。

===Original===
A racking cough was one of the peculiarities of my friend, and determined to assume the character in toto, I allowed myself to startle the silence now and then with a series of gasps and chokings that whether agreeable or not, certainly were of a character to show that I had no desire to conceal my presence from those I had come among. Indeed it was my desire to acquaint them as fully and as soon as possible with the fact of their having a neighbor: a weak-eyed half-alive innocent to be sure, but yet a neighbor who would keep his door open night and day﻿—for the warmth of the hall of course﻿—and who with the fretful habit of an old man who had once been a gentleman and a beau, went rambling about through the hall speaking to those he met and expecting a civil word in return. When he was not rambling or coughing he made architectural monsters out of cardboard, wherewith to tempt the pennies out of the pockets of unwary children, an employment that kept him chained to a small table in the centre of his room directly opposite the open door.

As I expected I had scarcely given way to three separate fits of coughing, when the door next me opened with a jerk and a rough voice called out,

“Who’s that making all that to do about here? If you don’t stop that infernal noise in a hurry﻿—”

A soft voice interrupted him and he drew back. “I will go see,” said those gentle tones, and Luttra Blake, for I knew it was she before the skirt of her robe had advanced beyond the door, stepped out into the hall.

I was yet bent over my work when she paused before me. The fact is I did not dare look up, the moment was one of such importance to me.

===Chinese===
我那位朋友有个标志，便是撕心的咳嗽；我既打定主意把这个角色扮个十足，便不时由着自己，用一连串的干喘和噎气去惊破寂静——这些声音入不入耳且不论，至少足以表明：我对我要混迹其间的这些人，并无掩藏行迹之意。其实我正想让他们越快越周全地知道隔壁添了个邻居：一个眼神发虚、半死不活的老好人，可到底是个邻居；这人要昼夜敞着房门——自然是为了楼道里那点暖和——又带着老人家做惯了绅士、做惯了浪荡子的那点烦躁脾气，在楼道里踱来踱去，逢人搭话，指望人家也还他一句客气话。他若不是在踱步或咳嗽，便用硬纸板搭造些建筑怪物，哄那些不提防的孩子们掏出口袋里的铜子——这份营生把他拴在屋子正中一张小桌前，正对着那扇敞开的门。

果然不出所料，我才咳足了三轮，隔壁的门猛地拉开，一个粗嗓子吼道：

「是谁在这儿穷折腾？你要不赶快收了那要命的动静——」

一个柔和的声音拦住了他，他缩了回去。「我去看看。」那温存的嗓音说道。走出来的正是卢特拉·布莱克——不等她裙裾挪出门槛，我已认出是她——她走进楼道，站到了我跟前。

她在我面前停下时，我还弓身做着我的活计。实情是我不敢抬头；这一刻于我干系太大。

===Original===
“You have a dreadful cough,” said she with that low ring of sympathy in her voice that goes unconsciously to the heart. “Is there no help for it?”

I pushed back my work, drew my hand over my eyes, (I did not need to make it tremble) and glanced up. “No,” said I with a shake of my head, “but it is not always so bad. I beg your pardon, miss, if it disturbs you.”

She threw back the shawl which she had held drawn tightly over her head, and advanced with an easy gliding step close to my side. “You do not disturb *me*, but my father is﻿—is, well a trifle cross sometimes, and if he should speak up a little harsh now and then, you must not mind. I am sorry you are so ill.”

What is there in some women’s look, some women’s touch that more than all beauty goes to the heart and subdues it. As she stood there before me in her dark worsted dress and coarse shawl, with her locks simply braided and her whole person undignified by art and ungraced by ornament, she seemed just by the power of her expression and the witchery of her manner, the loveliest woman I had ever beheld.

“You are veree kind, veree good,” I murmured, half ashamed of my disguise, though it was assumed for the purpose of rescuing her. “Your sympathy goes to my heart.” Then as a deep growl of impatience rose from the room at my side, I motioned her to go and not irritate the man who seemed to have such control over her.

===Chinese===
「您咳得好凶。」她说，嗓音里带着那一种低低的同情，不由分说直透人心。「就没个法子治么？」

我推开活计，一只手抹过眼睛（不必装，它自己就在抖），抬眼一望。「没有法子。」我摇着头说，「不过也不总这样凶。小姐，若搅扰了您，我告罪。」

她把紧紧裹在头上的一方披巾向后一掀，迈着从容轻滑的步子挨到我身旁。「我倒不嫌*扰*，是我父亲——他呢，有时脾气坏些；他若偶尔说话冲了您，您千万别放在心上。您病成这样，我心里难受。」

有些女子的眼色、有些女子的触碰里，究竟藏着什么，竟能越过一切美貌，直取人心，把人心收伏。她立在我面前，一身深色粗呢衣裙，一条粗披巾，发辫编得素朴，通身上下不见妆点、不假雕饰；单凭那表情的力量、那举止里的魔力，她已是我生平所见最可爱的女子。

「您客气得很，好得很。」我喃喃道，对自己的乔装几乎生出愧意，虽然这乔装原是为搭救她而设。「您的体恤，暖到我心窝里了。」这时我身旁那间屋里升起一声不耐烦的深吼，我便作势请她走开，别去招惹那个似乎把她辖制得死死的人。

===Original===
“In a minute,” answered she, “first tell me what you are making.”

So I told her and in the course of telling, let drop such other facts about my fancied life as I wished to have known to her and through her to her father. She looked sweetly interested and more than once turned upon me that dark eye, of which I had heard so much, full of tears that were as much for me, scamp that I was, as for her own secret trouble. But the growls becoming more and more impatient she speedily turned to go, repeating, however, as she did so,

“Now remember what I say, you are not to be troubled if they do speak cross to you. They make noise enough themselves sometimes, as you will doubtless be assured of tonight.”

And the lips which seemed to have grown stiff and cold with her misery, actually softened into something like a smile.

The nod which I gave her in return had the solemnity of a vow in it.

===Chinese===
「再待一刻。」她答道，「先告诉我，您做的是什么。」

我便告诉了她；趁着讲述的工夫，又把我想让她知道、并想借她的口传到她父亲耳朵里的那些我那虚构身世的零碎，一点点漏了出来。她听得一脸温存的关切，不止一次朝我转过那只我所久闻的黑眼睛，眼里噙着泪——那泪一半是为我这个无赖，一半也是为她自己的隐痛。可那一声声吼越来越不耐烦，她只得赶快转身要走，临走还叮嘱了一句：

「记着我的话：他们若说话冲撞了您，您不要烦恼。他们自己有时也闹得天响，今儿夜里您保准能见识到。」

那两片似乎已被苦难冻僵的唇，竟真软化成了一点近乎微笑的样子。

我还她的一点头里，带着立誓一般的郑重。

===Original===
My mind thus assured as to the correctness of my suspicions, and the way thus paved to the carrying out of my plans, I allowed some few days to elapse without further action on my part. My motive was to acquaint myself as fully as possible with the habits and ways of these two desperate men, before making the attempt to capture them upon which so many interests hung. For while I felt it would be highly creditable to my sagacity, as well as valuable to my reputation as a detective, to restore these escaped convicts in any way possible into the hands of justice, my chief ambition after all was to so manage the affair as to save the wife of Mr. Blake, not only from the consequences of their despair, but from the publicity and scandal attendant upon the open arrest of two heavily armed men. Strategy, therefore, rather than force was to be employed, and strategy to be successful must be founded upon the most thorough knowledge of the matter with which one has to deal. Three days, then, did I give to the acquiring of that knowledge, the result of which was the possession of the following facts.

1. That the landlady was right when she told me the girl was never left alone, one of the men, if not the father then the son, always remaining with her.

2. That while thus guarded, she was not so restricted but that she had the liberty of walking in the hall, though never for any length of time.

3. That the cross on the door seemed to possess some secret meaning connected with their presence in the house, it having been erased one evening when the whole three went out on some matter or other, only to be chalked on again when in an hour or so later, father and daughter returned alone.

4. That it was the father and not the son who made such purchases as were needed, while it was the son and not the father who carried on whatever operations they had on hand; nightfall being the favorite hour for the one and midnight for the other; though it not infrequently happened that the latter sauntered out for a short time also in the afternoon, probably for the drink he could not go long without.

===Chinese===
我的疑心既已坐实，道路也已铺平，只等我放手行事，我便按兵不动，又过了几天。我的用意，是在动手擒拿之前——多少人的利害都系在这一捉上——先把这些亡命之徒的习性门路摸个通透。因为，虽说把这两个越狱的逃犯以任何方式交还法网，于我的明察是极大的光彩，于我侦探的名声也是极厚的资本，但我真正的抱负，终究是把这场官司办得能救下布莱克先生的太太，不但使她免遭他们绝望之下的毒手，也使她免受两个持枪悍夫被公然逮捕所要招来的喧腾与丑闻。所以要用的不是力，而是计；而计要成，必得先把对手的底细摸到最透。于是我用三天工夫去摸这份底细，结果到手了以下几条事实。

一、房东太太说得不错，那姑娘从不被单独留下，总有一个男人守着她——不是父亲，便是儿子。

二、她虽这样被看管着，倒也未曾被拘死，还可以在楼道里走走，只是从不长久。

三、门上那个十字似乎同他们住在此处有着某种隐秘的干系：有一晚三人一同出门办事，那十字便被擦去；约莫一个钟头后，父女二人独自回来，它又被画了上去。

四、采买所需，出面的是父亲而不是儿子；他们手头正在办的那些勾当，动手的则是儿子而不是父亲。前者的好时辰是黄昏，后者是半夜；不过后者也常在午后溜出去一小会儿，多半是为他那片刻离不得的酒。

===Original===
5. That they were men of great strength but little alertness; the stray glimpses I had had of them, revealing a breadth of back that was truly formidable, if it had not been joined to a heaviness of motion that proclaimed a certain stolidity of mind that was eminently in our favor.

How best to use these facts in the building up of a matured plan of action, was, then, the problem. By noon of a certain day I believed it to have been solved, and reluctant as I was to leave the spot of my espionage even for the hour or two necessary to a visit to headquarters, I found myself compelled to do so. Packing up in a small basket I had for the purpose, the little articles I had been engaged during the last few days in making, I gave way to a final fit of coughing so hollow and sepulchral in its tone, that it awoke a curse from the next room deep as the growl of a wild beast, and still continuing, finally brought Luttra to the door with that look of compassion on her face that always called up a flush to my cheek whether I wished it or no.

“Ah, Monsieur, I am afraid your cough is very bad today. O I see; you have been getting ready to go out﻿—”

“Come back here,” broke in a heavy voice from the room she had left. “What do you mean by running off to palaver with that old rascal every time he opens his —﻿—﻿— battery of a cough?”

A smile that went through me like the cut of a knife, flashed for a moment on her face.

===Chinese===
五、这两人力气极大，机警却少；我零零星星瞥见的几眼，显出他们背膀之宽，委实骇人；好在与之相随的还有行动的迟重，说明头脑有一定的木然——这于我们大是有利。

如何把这些事实用起来，搭成一套成熟的行动计划，便成了课题。到某一天的正午，我自信课题已经解出；虽则我舍不得离开侦查的岗位，哪怕只是去一趟总部所需的那一两个钟头，也觉得难舍，却终究不得不去。我把连日来制成的小玩意收进一只备下的提篮，又发出一阵最后的咳嗽，那咳声空空洞洞，如出墓穴，激得隔壁房间咒骂了一声，深沉得像野兽的低吼；咳声还不肯罢休，终于把卢特拉引到了门口，她脸上带着那副怜悯的神情——无论我愿不愿意，那神情总要唤起我颊上的一阵发烧。

「啊，先生，我怕您今天咳得厉害。哦，我明白了；您这是收拾好了要出门——」

「回来！」她离开的那间屋里，一个沉浊的声音横插进来。「那老流氓一开他那一门子破咳嗽，你就跑过去跟他啰嗦，你是什么意思？」

一个微笑在她脸上一闪，却像一刀似的穿过我的身子。

===Original===
“My father is in one of his impatient moods,” said she, “you had better go. I hope you will be successful,” she murmured, glancing wistfully at my basket.

“What is that?” again came thundering on our ears. “Successful? What are you two up to?” And we heard the rough clatter of advancing steps.

“Go,” said she; “you are weak and old; and when you come back, try and not cough.” And she gave me a gentle push towards the door.

“When I come back,” I began, but was forced to pause, the elder Schoenmaker having by this time reached the open doorway where he stood frowning in upon us in a way that made my heart stand still for her.

“What are you two talking about?” said he; “and what have you got in your basket there?” he continued with a stride forward that shook the floor.

===Chinese===
「我父亲又犯他那烦躁性子了，」她说，「您还是走吧。愿您顺利。」她望着我那提篮，低声喃喃，眼里满是盼望。

「那是什么？」又一声雷似地灌进我们耳朵。「顺利？你们两个捣什么鬼？」我们听见一阵粗重的脚步逼来。

「走吧，」她说，「您又弱又老；回来的时候，千万忍着些，别咳嗽。」她朝门口轻轻推了我一把。

「等我回来——」我刚开口，却不得不顿住；老舍恩马克这时已走到敞开的门口，站在那里朝我们皱眉而视，那副模样，叫我的心为她悬在了半空。

「你们两个讲什么？」他说，「你篮子里装的又是什么？」他跨前一步，地板都震了震。

===Original===
“Only some little toys that he has been making, and is now going out to sell,” was her low answer given with a quick deprecatory gesture such as I doubt if she ever used for herself.

“Nothing more?” asked he in German with a red glare in the eye he turned towards her.

“Nothing more,” replied she in the same tongue. “You may believe me.”

He gave a deep growl and turned away. “If there was,” said he, “you know what would happen.” And unheeding the wild keen shudder that seized her at the word, making her insensible for the moment to all and everything about her, he laid one heavy hand upon her slight shoulder and led her from the room.

I waited no longer than was necessary to carry my feeble and faltering steps appropriately down the stairs, to reach the floor below and gain the landlady’s presence.

===Chinese===
「不过是他做的一些小玩意，如今要拿出去卖。」她低声答道，飞快地做了一个告罪的姿势——那样的姿势，我疑心她为自己也未必肯用。

「没有别的了么？」他用德语问，转向她的那只眼睛里燃着一团红光。

「没有别的了。」她用同样的语言答道，「你可以信我。」

他低吼一声，转过身去。「若有别的，」他说，「你知道会怎样。」那两个字一出，一阵狂乱的剧颤攫住了她，使她一时对身外的一切都浑然不觉；他却浑不理会，把一只沉重的手搭上她纤瘦的肩头，领着她出了房间。

我也只等了必要的工夫，便提着我那衰朽蹒跚的步子，像模像样地挪下楼梯，下到下面一层，来到房东太太跟前。

===Original===
“Do you go up,” said I, “and sit on those stairs till I come back. If you hear the least cry of pain or sound of struggle from that young girl’s room, do you call at once for help. I will have a policeman standing on the corner below.”

The good woman nodded and proceeded at once to take up her workbasket. “Lucky there’s a window up there, so I can see,” I heard her mutter. “I’ve no time to throw away even on deeds of charity.”

Notwithstanding which precaution, I was in constant anxiety during my absence; an absence necessarily prolonged as I had to stop and explain matters to the Superintendent, as well as hunt up Mr. Gryce and get his consent to assist me in the matter of the impending arrest.

I found the latter in his own home and more than enthusiastic upon the subject.

“Well,” said he after I had informed him of the discoveries I had made, “the fates seem to prosper you in this. *I* have not received an inkling of light upon the matter since I parted from you at Mr. Blake’s house. By the way I saw that gentleman this morning and I tell you we will find him a grateful man if this affair can be resolved satisfactorily.”

===Chinese===
「你上楼去，」我说，「坐在楼梯口等我回来。若听见那年轻姑娘房里有一点痛呼、一丝挣扎的声响，你就立刻喊人救助。我会叫一名警察守在下头的街角。」

好心的女人点点头，立刻起身去收拾她的针线篮。「幸亏上头有个窗户，我还看得见，」我听见她咕哝，「连行善的工夫，我也一丝丢不起呢。」

尽管有这一道防范，我人不在，心却始终悬着；这一趟也不得不多耽搁——我得停下向总监禀明情由，还得寻着格赖斯先生，求得他肯襄助这场迫在眉睫的逮捕。

我在格赖斯先生自己家里寻着了他；提起这件事，他岂止热心而已。

「好啊，」我向他讲明我的种种发现之后，他说道，「这一回，命运女神像是偏要成全你。自从在布莱克先生府上与你分手，*我*在这案子上可没得到半点亮。对了，今早我见过那位先生；我告诉你，这桩事若能妥帖了结，我们会发现他是个知恩图报的人。」

===Original===
“That is good,” said I, “gratitude is what we want.” Then shortly, “Perhaps it is no more than our duty to let him know that his wife is safe and under my eye; though I would by no means advocate his knowing just how near him she is, till the moment comes when he is wanted, or we shall have a lover’s impetuosity to deal with as well as all the rest.” Then with a hurried remembrance of a possible contingency, went on to say, “But, by the way, in case we should need the cooperation of Mrs. Blake in what we have before us, you had better get a line written in French from Mrs. Daniels, expressive of her belief in Mr. Blake’s present affection for his wife. The latter will not otherwise trust us, or understand that we are to be obeyed in whatever we may demand. Let it be unsigned and without names in case of accident; and if the housekeeper don’t understand French, tell her to get someone to help her that does, only be sure that the handwriting employed is her own.”

Mr. Gryce seemed to perceive the wisdom of this precaution and promised to procure me such a note by a certain hour, after which I related to him the various other details of the capture such as I had planned it, meeting to my secret gratification an unqualified approval that went far towards alleviating that wound to my pride which I had received from him in the beginning of this affair.

“Let all things proceed as you have determined, and we shall accomplish something that it will be a lifelong satisfaction to remember,” said he; “but you must be prepared for some twist of the screw which you do not anticipate. I never knew anything to go off just as one prognosticates it must, except once,” he added thoughtfully, “and then it was with a surprise attached to it that well nigh upset me notwithstanding all my preparations.”

“You won a great success that day,” remarked I. “I hope the fates will be as propitious to me tomorrow. Failure now would break my heart.”

“But you won’t fail,” exclaimed he. “I myself am resolved to see you through this matter with credit.”

===Chinese===
「那好，」我说，「我们要的正是知恩图报。」随后又简短地说：「他太太平安、在我眼皮底下，让他知道这一点，或许不过是我们分内的事；只是我决不主张他此刻就知道她离他这样近——非得等到用得着他的时刻不可，不然，我们除了眼前这一切，还得应付一个情人的性急。」接着忽然记起一件可能的意外，又说道：「不过，对了，万一往下的步骤里需要布莱克太太合作，你最好从丹尼尔斯太太那里讨一行法文的字条，写明她相信布莱克先生如今是爱着他太太的。不然，布莱克太太便不会信我们，也不会明白：凡我们有所要求，都须照办。字条不要署名，也不要写名字，以防万一；那位管家若不懂法文，叫她寻个懂的人帮忙，只消务必是她亲笔。」

格赖斯先生似乎看出了这层谨慎里的道理，答应在指定时刻之前替我把这样一封短信弄来。随后我又向他讲了逮捕的其余各项细节，全照我拟定的那样；他听后毫无保留地一律赞成——这暗暗满足了我的快意，也大大抚平了本案之初他给我的自尊所受的那一处伤。

「一切照你定的去办吧，我们必能办成一桩想起来便终身快慰的事。」他说，「不过你还得防着什么料想不到的节外生枝。我生平只知道事事都照人预料的路子走，那可从来没有过——除了一回，」他若有所思地补上一句，「而且那一回还附着一场意外，任凭我有万全的预备，也险些把我掀了个跟头。」

「那一回您可是大获全胜。」我说，「但愿明天命运女神也这样眷顾我。此刻若是失败，我的心都要碎了。」

「你不会失败的。」他叫道，「这一回，我亲自打定主意，要看着你有头有脸地把它办成。」

===Original===
And in this assurance I returned to my lodgings where I found the landlady sitting where I had left her, darning her twenty-third sock.

“I have to mend for a dozen men and three boys,” said she, “and the boys are the worst by a heap sight. Look at that, will you,” holding up a darn with a bit of stocking attached. “*That* hole was made playing shinny.”

I uttered my condolences and asked if any sound or disturbance had reached her ears from above.

“O no, all is right up there; I’ve scarcely heard a whisper since you’ve been gone.”

I gave her a pat on the chin scarcely consistent with my aged and tottering mien and proceeded to shamble painfully to my room.

===Chinese===
怀着这句话，我回到寓所，见房东太太还坐在我离开时的老地方，织补她的第二十三只袜子。

「我得给十二个男人、三个男孩缝补，」她说，「男孩的袜子破得才叫厉害。你瞧瞧这个，」她举起一处连着半片袜子的补丁，「*那个*洞，是打曲棍球打出来的。」

我道了几句慰问，问她可曾听见头顶上有什么声响或骚动。

「嗐，没有，上头一切都好；你走了以后，我连一句人语都没听见。」

我在她的下巴上轻轻拍了一下——这同我那老态龙钟、步履摇摇的身份颇不相称——随后便一步一拖、苦着脸挪回自己房里去了。

## XVII The Capture / 十七 抓捕

===Original===
Promptly next morning at the designated hour, came the little note promised me by Mr. Gryce. It was put in my hand with many sly winks by the landlady herself, who developed at this crisis quite an adaptation for, if not absolute love of intrigue and mystery. Glancing over it﻿—it was unsealed﻿—and finding it entirely unintelligible, I took it for granted it was all right and put it by till chance, or if that failed, strategy, should give me an opportunity to communicate with Mrs. Blake. An hour passed; the doors of their rooms remained unclosed. A half hour more dragged its slow minutes away, and no sound had come from their precincts save now and then a mumbled word of parley between the father and son, a short command to the daughter, or a not-to-be-restrained oath of annoyance from one or both of the heavy-limbed brutes as something was said or done to disturb them in their indolent repose. At last my impatience was to be no longer restrained. Rising, I took a bold resolution. If the mountain would not come to Muhammad, Muhammad would go to the mountain. Taking my letter in the hand, I deliberately proceeded to the door marked with the ominous red cross and knocked.

A surprised snarl from within, followed by a sudden shuffling of feet as the two men leaped upright from what I presume had been a recumbent position, warned me to be ready to face defiance if not the fury of despair; and curbing with a determined effort the slight sinking of heart natural to a man of my make on the threshold of a very doubtful adventure, I awaited with as much apparent unconcern as possible, the quick advance of that light foot which seemed to be ready to perform all the biddings of these hardened wretches, much as it shrunk from following in the ways of their infamy.

“Ah miss,” said I, as the door opened revealing in the gap her white face clouded with some new and sudden apprehension, “I beg your pardon but I am an old man, and I got a letter today and my eyes are so weak with the work I’ve been doing that I cannot read it. It is from someone I love, and would you be so kind as to read off the words for me and so relieve an old man from his anxiety.”

The murmur of suspicion behind her, warned her to throw wide open the door. “Certainly,” said she, “if I can,” taking the paper in her hand.

“Just let me get a squint at that first,” said a sullen voice behind her; and the youngest of the two Schoenmakers stepped forward and tore the paper out of her grasp.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，到了约定的时刻，格赖斯先生许我的那封短信果然来了。是房东太太亲手交到我手里的，还配上许多诡秘的眼色——到了这个节骨眼上，这位太太竟颇显出几分对阴谋密事的适应，若不说嗜好的话。我扫了一眼——信没有封口——见它通篇不知所云，我便认定它没错，收在一旁，只等机缘、或机缘不成便用计谋，给我一个同布莱克太太通消息的机会。一个钟头过去了；他们的房门始终没开。又挨过半个钟头，那半个钟头的分针磨磨蹭蹭地爬，他们的住处仍无一点声息，只偶尔飘出父子俩含混的交头接语，对女儿的一句简短吩咐，或是那两个粗手粗脚的蠢物中的一个或两个，被什么扰了懒觉时，憋不住的一声咒骂。末了，我的焦躁再也按捺不住。我站起身，打了一个大胆的主意。山不肯就穆罕默德，穆罕默德便去就山。我手里拿着那封信，从容走向那扇带着不祥红十字的门，敲了敲。

门里一声惊怒的咆哮，接着两脚一阵急促的蹭动——那两个人想必原是躺着的，此刻一跃而起——这提醒我做好准备，迎接的纵不是绝望的狂怒，也得是顽抗；我屏住心头那一点自然的发虚——我这副身量的人，站在一桩吉凶未卜的冒险门槛上，免不了要虚一虚——竭力装出满不在乎的样子，等待那双轻捷的脚步快步前来。这双脚似乎已惯于听凭那两个狠毒恶棍的差遣，尽管它多么畏缩，不肯追随他们走上那条无耻的路。

「啊，小姐，」门开了，门缝里露出她那张白脸，罩着一层新起的、突如其来的惊惶，我说道，「恕我冒昧。我是个上了年纪的人，今天收到一封信，可我的眼睛做活计做得发了花，认不得字了。这是我一个亲近人的来信，可否劳驾您念给我听听，也免得我这老头子悬着心。」

她背后一阵嘀嘀咕咕的疑声，催得她把门整个敞开。「当然可以，」她说，「只要我念得来。」说着把纸接在手里。

「先让我瞅一眼。」她身后一个瓮声瓮气的声音说；舍恩马克父子中年轻的那一个抢上前来，一把将那纸从她手里夺了过去。

===Original===
“You are too suspicious,” murmured she, looking after him with the first assumption of that air of power and determination which I had heard so eloquently described by the man who loved her. “There is nothing in those lines which concerns us; let me have them back.”

“You hold your tongue,” was the brutal reply as the rough man opened the folded paper and read or tried to read what was written within. “Blast it! it’s French,” was his slow exclamation after a moment spent in this way. “See,” and he thrust it towards his father who stood frowning heavily a few feet off.

“Of course, it’s French,” cried the girl. “Would you write a note in English to father there? The man’s friends are French like himself, and must write in their own language.”

“Here take it and read it out,” commanded her father; “and mind you tell us what it means. I’ll have nothing going on here that I don’t understand.”

“Read me the French words first, miss,” said I. “It is my letter and I want to know what my friend has to say to me.”

===Chinese===
「你疑心太重了。」她低声道，目送着他，头一回摆出那副威严决断的气派——爱她的那个男人，曾向我绘声绘色地描述过这气派。「这几行字与我们无干；还给我。」

「闭上你的嘴。」这是粗暴的回答；那粗汉展开折起的信纸，读着、或试图读着里面写的字。「见鬼！是法文。」他这样折腾了半晌，才慢吞吞地冒出这一句。「瞧。」他把信朝几步外沉眉立着的父亲那边一戳。

「当然是法文。」姑娘叫道，「不然，人家会给那边那位老人家写英文信么？那人的朋友同他一样是法国人，自然要用他们自己的文字写。」

「拿去，念出来。」她父亲喝道，「记住，得告诉我们它是什么意思。这里头不许有一样我不明白的事。」

「先给我念念那些法国字吧，小姐。」我说，「信是我的，我总该知道我的朋友要对我说什么。」

===Original===
Nodding at me with a gentle look, she cast her eyes on the paper and began to read:

> “Calmez vous, mon amie, il vous aime et il vous cherche. Dans quatre heures vous serez heureuse. Allons du courage, et surtout soyez maitre de vous même.”

“Thanks!” I exclaimed in a calm matter-of-fact way as I perceived the sudden tremor that seized her as she recognized the handwriting and realized that the words were for her. “My friend says he will pay my week’s rent and bids me be at home to receive him,” said I, turning upon the two ferocious faces peering over her shoulder, with a look of meek unsuspiciousness in my eye, that in a theatre would have brought down the house.

“Is that what those words say, you?” asked the father, pointing over her shoulder to the paper she held.

“I will translate for you word by word what it says,” replied she, nerving herself for the crisis till her face was like marble, though I could see she could not prevent the gleam of secret rapture that had visited her, from flashing fitfully across it. “*Calmez vous, mon amie.* Do not be afraid, my friend. *Il vous aime et il vous cherche.* He loves you and is hunting for you. *Dans quatre heures vous serez heureuse.* In four hours you will be happy. *Allons du courage, et surtout soyez maitre de vous même.* Then take courage and above all preserve your self-possession. It is the French way of expressing one’s self,” observed she. “I am glad your friend is disposed to help you,” she continued, giving me back the letter with a smile. “I am afraid you needed it.”

===Chinese===
她朝我温存地点了点头，垂眼看着信纸，念了起来：

> 「定下心来，我的朋友，他爱着你，他正寻访你。四个钟头之后，你就要得到幸福了。鼓起勇气，尤其要守住你自己。」

「多谢！」我平静地、就事论事地喊了一声；我已看见她认出笔迹、明白这些话原是为她而写时，蓦地攫住她的那一阵战栗。「我的朋友说，他替我付这一礼拜的房钱，还叫我守在家里等他。」我说着，掉头迎向那两张从她肩后逼视过来的凶脸，眼中一副温顺不疑的神气——这副神气若摆在戏台上，管教满堂喝彩。

「那些话是这么说的么，你？」父亲指着纸问，手指越过她的肩头。

「我给你们一字一句地译出来。」她答道，鼓足了劲应付这场关头，脸白得像大理石；可我仍看得出，那阵掠过她的隐秘的狂喜，还是一阵一阵在她脸上发亮。「*Calmez vous, mon amie*。定下心来，我的朋友。*Il vous aime et il vous cherche*。他爱着你，他正寻访你。*Dans quatre heures vous serez heureuse*。四个钟头之后，你就要得到幸福了。*Allons du courage, et surtout soyez maitre de vous même*。那么鼓起勇气，尤其要守住你自己。这是法国人表达意思的惯路。」她议论了一句，随后把信还给我，带着一个微笑。「您这位朋友肯帮您，我很高兴。」她接着说，「我怕您正需要人帮呢。」

===Original===
In a sort of maze I folded up the letter, bowed my very humble thanks to her and shuffled slowly back. The fact is I had no words; I was utterly dumbfounded. Halfway through that letter, with whose contents you must remember I was unacquainted, I would have given my whole chance of expected reward to have stopped her. Read out such words as those before these men! Was she crazy? But how naturally at the conclusion did she with a word make its language seem consistent with the meaning I had given it. With a fresh sense of my obligation to her, I hurried to my room, there to count out the minutes of another long hour in anxious expectation of her making that endeavor to communicate with me, which her new hopes and fears must force her to feel almost necessary to her existence. At length, my confidence in her was rewarded. Coming out into the hall, she hurried past my door, her finger on her lip. I immediately rose and stood on the threshold with another paper in my hand, which I had prepared against this opportunity. As she glided back, I put it in her hand, and warning her with a look not to speak, resumed my usual occupation. The words I had written were as follows:

> At or as near the time as possible of your brother’s going out, you are to come to this room wrapped in an extra skirt and with your shawl over your head. Leave the skirt and shawl behind you, and withdraw at once to the room at the head of the stairs. You are not to speak, and you are not to vary from the plan thus laid down. Your brother and father are to be arrested, whether or no; but if you will do as this commands, they will be arrested without bloodshed and without shame to *one you know*.

Her face while she read these lines, was a study, but I dared not soften toward it. Dropping the paper from her hand, she gave me one inquiring look. But I pointed determinedly to the words lying upward on the floor, and would listen to no appeal. My resolve had its effect. Bowing her head with a sorrowful gesture, she laid her hand on her heart, looked up and glided from the room. I took up that paper and tore it into bits.

And now for the first time since I had been in the house, I closed the door of my room. I had a part to perform that rendered the dropping of my disguise indispensable. The old French artist had finished his work, and henceforth must merge into Q, the detective. Shortly before two o’clock my assistants began to arrive. First, Mr. Gryce appeared on the scene and was stowed away in a large room on the other side of mine. Next, two of the most agile, as well as muscular men in the force who, thanks to having taken off their shoes in the lower hall, gained the same refuge without awakening the suspicions of those we were anxious to surprise. Lastly, the landlady who went into the closet to which I had bidden Mrs. Blake retire after leaving in my room the articles I had mentioned.

All was now ready and waiting for the departure of the youngest Schoenmaker. Would he disappoint us and remain at home that day? Had any suspicions been awakened in the stolid breasts of these men, that would serve to make them more watchful than usual against running unnecessary risks? No; at or near the time for the clock to strike two, their door opened and the tread of a lumbering foot was heard in the hall. On it came, passing my room with a rude stamping that gradually grew less distinct as the hardy rough went down the corridor, brushing the wall behind which Mr. Gryce and his men lay concealed with his thick cane, and even stopping to light his pipe in front of the small apartment where cowered our good landlady with her eternal basket of mending in her lap.

===Chinese===
我恍恍惚惚地把信折好，向她深深弓身道了谢，一步一蹭地退了回去。实情是我无话可说；我完全怔住了。那封信念到一半——您总记得，信里写的什么我原不知道——那一刻叫我拿预期酬金的全部指望去换她停口，我也肯。当着这两个男人的面，念出这样的一些话来！她疯了么？可是到了末尾，她只用一个巧妙的说法，便教信上的言语同我方才编的意思严丝合缝，何等自然。我怀着对她新添的一重感激，赶紧回到自己房中，在那里掰着指头，数又一个漫长钟头的分分秒秒，焦灼地盼她来作那一次同我通消息的尝试——她那新生的希望与恐惧，必定逼得她觉得此举几乎性命交关。终于，我对她的信心得了报偿。她走进楼道，匆匆掠过我的门前，一根手指抵在唇上。我立刻起身，手里拿着另一张纸，站在门槛上——那纸我早已备下，就等这个机会。她滑回来的当口，我把它塞进她手里，并用眼色警告她莫开口，随后照旧做我的活计。我写的是这样几句话：

> 「趁你兄弟出门的时候，或尽量贴近那个时辰，你要披上一条多余的裙子、头上搭着披巾，到这间屋里来。把裙子和披巾留下，即刻退到楼梯顶头的那间屋里去。不许开口，也不许更改这样定下的步骤。你的兄弟和父亲横竖要被捉拿；但你若照这纸条所吩咐的做，他们被拿时便可以不见血，也可以不给*你知道的那个人*丢脸。」

她读这几行字时，那张脸真够人研玩的；可我不敢对它软下心肠。她让纸从手里落下去，给了我一记探询的眼色。我却决然指着地上字迹朝上的那张纸，什么哀求也不理。我的决绝见了效。她黯然低下头，把一只手按在胸口，抬眼一望，悄然滑出了房间。我拾起那张纸，撕成了碎片。

于是，自住进这宅子以来，我头一回关上了自己房门。我有一场戏要演，非卸下乔装不可。老法国画家的活计做完了，从此得归并于侦探Q。快到两点钟时，我的帮手们陆续到了。头一个是格赖斯先生出场，安置在我屋子另一侧的一间大屋里。接着是警队里两个最矫捷、也最有力气的人，幸而他们早在楼下过道里脱了鞋，得以同样悄没声地进了那处藏身之所，没有惊动我们一心要出其不意的那些人。最后是房东太太，她把几样我点过的东西留在我的屋里之后，便钻进我先前吩咐布莱克太太退避的那间小屋。

如今万事俱备，只等小舍恩马克出门。他会叫我们失望、那天赖在家里么？这两个木然的家伙胸中，可曾起了什么疑心，教他们比平常更加提防、不敢冒无谓的险？没有；快到时钟敲两点、或恰在敲两点的时候，他们的门开了，楼道里响起一阵滞重的脚步。脚步过来了，过我的门口时带着一阵粗野的踏地声，渐渐远了——那硬朗的粗汉顺着走廊走下去，一路用粗手杖扫着墙根，那墙后面正藏着格赖斯先生和他的人；他甚至在我们好心的房东太太蜷伏着的那间小屋门前停下来点烟斗，她的膝上还搁着那只永远不离的针线篮。

===Original===
At length all was quiet, and throwing open my door, I withdrew into a small closet connected with my room, to wait with indescribable impatience, the appearance of Mrs. Blake. She came in a very few minutes, remained for an instant, and departed, leaving behind her as I had requested, the skirt and shawl in which she had left her father’s presence. I at once endued myself in these articles of apparel﻿—taking care to draw the shawl well over my head﻿—and with a pocket handkerchief to my face, (a proceeding made natural enough by the sneeze which at that very moment I took care should assail me) walked boldly back to the room from which she had just come.

The door was of course ajar, and as I swung it open with as near a simulation of her manner as possible, the vision of her powerful father lolling on a bench directly before me, offered anything but an encouraging spectacle to my eyes. But doubling myself almost together with as ladylike an *atch-ee* as my masculine nostrils would allow, I succeeded in closing the door and reaching a low stool by the window without calling from him anything worse than a fretful “I hope you are not going to bark too.”

I did not reply to this of course, but sat with my face turned towards the street in an attitude which I hoped would awaken his attention sufficiently to cause him to get up and come over to my side. For as he sat face to the door it would be impossible to take him by surprise, and that, now that I saw what a huge and muscular creature he was, seemed to me to be the only safe method before us. But, whether from the sullenness of his disposition or the very evident laziness of the moment, he manifested no disposition to move, and hearing or thinking I did, the stealthy advance of Mr. Gryce and his companions down the hall, I allowed myself to give way to a suppressed exclamation, and leaning forward, pressed my forehead against the pane of glass before me as if something of absorbing interest had just taken place in the street beneath.

His fears at once took alarm. Bounding up with a curse, he strode towards me, muttering,

“What’s up now? What’s that you are looking at?” reaching my side just as Mr. Gryce and his two men softly opened the door and with a quick leap threw their arms about him, closing upon him with a force he could not resist, desperate as he was and mighty in the huge strength of an unusually developed muscular organization.

===Chinese===
末了一切归于寂静。我敞开房门，自己退进与我屋子相通的一间小壁橱，怀着难以形容的急躁，等候布莱克太太现身。不几分钟她来了，待了一瞬，又去了，照我的嘱咐，把她离开父亲时穿的那条裙子和那方披巾留了下来。我立刻把这几样衣装穿戴到自己身上——留意把披巾严严地罩上头顶——又拿一块手帕掩住脸（这时我恰如其分地让自己打了个喷嚏，这动作便显得再自然不过），大摇大摆地走回她方才出来的那间屋去。

门自然是虚掩的；我尽量模仿着她的姿态推门进去，眼前她那强壮的父亲正歪在一条长凳上——这景象落在我眼里，实在说不上叫人鼓舞。可我把身子几乎对折起来，从我这男性的鼻子里挤出一声尽量斯文的*阿嚏*，总算关上了门，挪到窗边一只矮凳上坐下，没引出他比一句烦躁话更凶的反应：「我指望你可别也要叫唤。」

这话我自然不答，只把脸朝着大街坐着，摆出一个姿势，指望能勾起他的注意，教他起身走到我这边来。因为他坐着时脸朝着门，要出其不意地拿他，是办不到的；如今亲眼见了这彪形大汉的身量筋肉，我更明白出其不意是我们面前唯一稳妥的法子。可是，不知是他性子里那股乖戾，还是眼下那明摆着的懒怠，他毫无起身的意思；我听见——或者自以为听见——格赖斯先生一行人悄悄沿楼道逼进，便由着自己发出一声压住的惊呼，身子前倾，把额头抵在面前的玻璃窗上，仿佛楼底下街上刚出了什么煞是好看的事。

他的疑心立刻炸了。他咒骂着一跃而起，朝我大步走来，嘴里咕哝着：

「又怎么了？你瞧什么呢？」话音刚到我跟前，格赖斯先生和他的两个人已轻轻推开了门，一跃而上，几条臂膀把他箍住，合围之力叫他无从抵拒——任凭他如何亡命，任凭他那副异乎寻常的筋骨里蕴着怎样洪荒的巨力。

===Original===
“You, you girl there, are to blame for this!” came mingled with curses from his lips, as with one huge pant he submitted to his captors. “Only let me get my hand well upon you once﻿—Damn it!” he suddenly exclaimed, dragging the whole three men forward in his effort to get his mouth down to my ear, “go and rub that sign out on the door or I’ll﻿—you know what I’ll do well enough. Do you hear?”

Rising, still with face averted, I proceeded to do what he asked. But in another moment seeing that he had been effectually bound and gagged, I took out the piece of red chalk I had kept in my pocket, and deliberately chalked it on again, after which operation I came back and took my seat as before on the low stool by the window.

The object now was to secure the second rascal in the same way we had the first; and for this purpose Mr. Gryce ordered the now helpless giant to be dragged into the adjoining small room formerly occupied by Mrs. Blake, where he and his men likewise took up their station leaving me to confront as best I might, the surprise and consternation of the one whose return we now awaited.

I did not shrink. With that brave woman’s garments drawn about me, something of her dauntless spirit seemed to invade my soul, and though I expected﻿—But let that come in its place, I am not here to interest you in myself or my selfish thoughts.

A half hour passed; he had never lingered away so long before, or so it seemed, and I was beginning to wonder if we should have to keep up this strain of nerve for hours, when the heavy tread was again heard in the hall, and with a blow of the fist that argued anger or a brutal impatience, he flung open the door and came in, I did not turn my head.

===Chinese===
「你，你这家的丫头，这事都怪你！」咒骂声里滚出他这样一句，他长喘一口，任凭捉他的人摆布。「只消叫我这只手好好逮着你一回——该死！」他忽然叫起来，拖着那三条大汉往前挣，只为把嘴凑到我耳边，「去，把门上那个记号擦掉，不然我要——你知道我要干什么，清楚得很。听见没有？」

我站起来，仍侧着脸，依言去做了。可转眼见他已被结结实实地捆住、堵住了嘴，我便掏出一直揣在口袋里的那段红粉笔，慢条斯理地把那记号又画了上去；这道工序完事，我踱回来，照旧在窗边那只矮凳上坐下。

眼下的目标，是照拿第一个恶棍的样子拿下第二个；为此，格赖斯先生吩咐把这条如今动弹不得的大汉拖进隔壁那间小屋——布莱克太太先前住的那间；他和他的手下也在那里各就各位，只留下我去面对我们所等候之人回来时的惊愕与仓皇，能怎样面对便怎样面对。

我没有退缩。裹着那位勇敢女子的衣裳，她的无畏气概仿佛也渗进了我的魂魄；虽说我料到——但那且待它到该来的地方再说，我在这里，原不是拿我自己和我那点私心杂念来博诸位一哂的。

半个钟头过去了；他从不曾在外面耽搁这么久——至少我这样觉得；我正开始疑心是否得把这绷紧的神经一连撑上几个钟头，楼道里又响起了那滞重的脚步；他一拳擂开了门，怒冲冲地、带着蛮横的不耐烦走进来。我没有回头。

===Original===
“Where’s father?” he growled, stopping where he was a foot or so from the door.

I shook my head with a slight gesture and remained looking out.

He brought his cane down on the floor with a thump. “What do you mean by sitting there staring out of the window like mad and not answering when I ask you a decent question?”

Still I made no reply.
